In order to avoid such problems in the future, the organisational reform provides that the supervisory authority may require that the decision-making process be postponed on a agenda item, and that twice. The second example is that the supervisory authority can agree with social security objectives within the framework of the Balanced Scorecard – which is a target control – which then limit the scope of self-management. All of us sitting here are jointly required to eliminate these injustices. And even if there is now a repatriation, namely on collective treaty negotiations, on social partnership, I hope that we can finally establish a legal basis so that not only 97 percent of all employees in that country, but the total 100 percent, all women and all men, on below-limits in this respect, can call for minimum standards. We have a huge task ahead of us, we have a long to-do list ahead of us. We must stand up to the women in this country. Women bear the main burden of child rearing and, accordingly, the main burden of most future work. It is about getting the next generation so far on the way to creating families – and this is not supposed to bring about any disadvantage in the career or in pension claims and no poverty in old age. It is enough if we finally start to stop structural discrimination against women in this country. As has been said, we are unfortunately going this way; my proposals in the committee would have provided for an immediate legal regime. The attempt to involve the social partners in the collective agreements is a delay for me, means another three months that women in our country lose in terms of their claims, despite everything I believe it must be possible to take this path together, because I am concerned that discrimination against women in the field of education finally ceases. There are also a few general problems that we have with pension funds which are not addressed at all. The situation today is: The employer selects the pension fund. The pension fund decides how much risk is taken into consideration, carries zero risk in the accounting itself, but gets legally determined, quite fixed rates for the administrative costs. Whether the risk is high or low is whether she's done well, whether she's been working well or not, she doesn't care, she gets her money very well. 100 percent of the risk is borne by employees, without any influence on whether or not risk-rich is involved. This problem will not be addressed at all. And one last sentence: We are now discussing matters from the Financial Committee, one concerning the previous debate, the second is now the issue of pension funds, and the third will be a double taxation agreement. Point Report of the Financial Committee on the Government Template (258 e.g._Other Organiser Agreements between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Kosovo on the elimination of double taxation in the areas of income taxes and on the prevention of tax reduction and circumvention, together with Protocol 325, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e14562	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. I give the floor to Mr Eßl. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e14589	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Finanzminister! We are discussing today an agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Kosovo on the elimination of double taxation in the areas of income tax and on preventing tax reduction and tax evasion. Why are we doing that? – Because there is more intense economic relations between Austria and Kosovo and because we want to develop these economic relations and strengthen cooperation in tax matters. Economic relations – I have mentioned them – are the way in which we export goods to Kosovo worth EUR 48.81 million. With reference to the year 2017, we import on the other hand goods worth 10.99 million euros. The main Austrian export goods were mechanical products and vehicles in 2017, but also food products: Vegetables, fruits, cereals, pastries and dairy products. Austrian companies are among the most important investors in Kosovo. More than 100 companies with Austrian capital are registered in Kosovo. The main payment flows are, on the one hand, interest payments related to loans from Austrian banks to companies in Kosovo and, on the other hand, deposits of persons resident in Kosovo from Austrian banks. The aim of this double taxation agreement is therefore to strengthen the development of economic relations between Austria and Kosovo and the extension of official assistance in accordance with the international standard in tax cooperation. The conclusion can be seen as an essential legal requirement for the deepening of economic relations between Austria and Kosovo and therefore corresponds to Austria's local policy intentions. There were two rounds of negotiations. The Minister of Finance has this agreement on 8 November. We should also approve this agreement here in Parliament by today's decision. The main content of this Agreement concerns the flow of information. At present, in the absence of an agreement with the Republic of Kosovo, no exchange of tax information can be carried out according to the international standard. By including a corresponding provision in the agreement, Austria fulfils its international obligations to implement this standard and in addition creates reliable framework conditions for citizens in these two countries who set up economic activities, creates fiscal transparency and regulates mutual assistance. Finally, it is also true that if this Convention is not implemented, there would also be negative accompanying effects for the economy. Finally, I would like to point out the financial implications: We expect tax revenues of EUR 68 million per year, but tax revenue of EUR 232 million per year. That is why it is also a good and an important agreement. I'm seeking approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e17999	Thank you, Mr President. – on the financial statements: We have, of course, taken to heart the criticism of the Court of Auditors and have reworked the financial statements. Structural reform: The multi-disputed savings potential of 1 billion euros can be offset by the reduction in staff and property costs of a total of 30 percent by 2023. The target control system shall ensure that the efficiency increase will make available free resources for the services. In addition, lower costs in the IT sector as well as the combination of data centres and pressure roads make savings possible. For the accusation, money is removed from the health system and large corporations and private insurance is given: It is not true that the costs of the treatment of accidents at work will no longer be replaced by the costs of the cash registers. Please read the government template! The flat-rate payment is converted to individual invoices. This means that AUVA replaces the costs actually incurred in connection with accidents at work of the ÖGK. On the cost of merger: I am surprised that the opposition already knows the fusion costs. In addition to the opposition, no one at this time knows how high these costs are, because this is the decision of the self-government. [[Abg. Drozda: The Court of Auditors...!]] On the co-imported performance deterioration: The opposition is intended to show me where the Social Insurance Organisation Act enacts on the right to benefit of sickness insurance. [[Abg. Knes: Court of Auditors!]] I cannot find such a provision, because the performance cannot be mentioned in this organisational reform. These are still defined by self-management. For the sake of accusation, employers decide on the benefits of employees: I think it is in the sense of social partnership to negotiate at the same level and to reflect this principle in self-management. Up to now, the members of the Board of Directors had the majority and the service providers in the control assembly. In the new Board of Directors, staff and service providers have the same weight. For the sake of accusation, self retention is introduced: Nothing changes on the system of self retention. No new restraints are introduced for workers and employees. Reduce benefits: As has already been said, there is no provision for a reduction in performance throughout the law. With regard to the privatisation of health facilities: I'm not going to privatise a health facility. This is even explicitly normized in the law. The patient's billion will have a centralization billion. The administrative burden, as the opposition represents, is incomplete. [[Abg. Knes: Court of Auditors!]] In fact, the administrative burden in the Austrian social security scheme is much higher. At present, certain expenses, such as the costs of the head of medical services, the benefits of health insurance, and not administrative expenses are included. The contribution auditors migrate to the financial sector: There is already a joint audit of local health telephones and finance. In the future, only more will examine the financial situation. However, the final decision always takes the Austrian health insurance fund. To claim that self-management is eliminated in other parts: The self-management is by no means disabled, it is only reduced. The reduction of the funds also logically reduces self-management. There is also the accusation that the main association will be smashed and should have seven different chairmen in five years: The main association is not broken down, but made slimmer in a umbrella association, and the principle of rotation has also proved successful in other areas, see also in the EU, in the Austrian Federal Council and in German social security schemes. [[Abg. Vogl: Trainer Bank!]] [[Abg. Klaus Uwe Feichtinger: As in any other group!]] Ladies and gentlemen! As a government, we guarantee that every Austrian and every Austrian will receive the health care they or he need. [[Congratulations with FPÖ and ÖVP.]] And now I come to answer your questions. [[Abg. Klaus Uwe Feichtinger:... six months!]] Question 1: Performance harmonisation within the Austrian Health Fund is provided for within this legislative period. On question 2: In the draft, such a one is very well planned, within the Austrian health insurance fund. Question 3: The patient billions of people are cumulated in the implementation of efficiency measures for four years to 2023, for example in the non-retirement of retirement social security staff or in the case of less expenditure on property. Question 4: In any event, I will not approve any additional burden, nor will the social security bodies and therefore the service representatives be responsible for this area. Question 5: The final scale of the merger costs is determined by the self-government as they determine the extent of the expenditure itself. On question 6: Fusion costs face short-, medium- and long-term efficiency gains. Question 7: The relatively high average age of social security staff is expected to increase in the next few years, about 30 percent in the next five years. In this context, it should also be mentioned that no merger-related terminations will occur by law. Question 8: Modern management is also achieved by improving efficiency, which is facilitated by structural reform. On the question 9: Talks have already taken place with the countries. A discussion has also been held with the three SPÖ-led states of Vienna, Carinthia and Burgenland, which have triggered the so-called consultation mechanism. The concerns raised have been scattered and resolved. Further discussions with all health professionals were invited for November. Question 10: The costs of health insurance in the countries will definitely be replaced, even if there is only one Austrian health insurance fund. The service obligation remains fully maintained, as the existing contracts with regard to medical services continue to apply. On the question 11: It is provided for by law to harmonise the right to contribute and benefit within the two new insurance institutions. Starting from 30. June 2020 will be reported to me every six months on the progress of this unification. Question 12: The Austrian health insurance fund and PVA cannot be compared with the BVAEB. The service structure of the BVAEB is different from that of PVA and ÖGK. In addition, the proportion of employees in the contributions in the BVAEB is higher than the share of the service provider. On the question 13: To date, the service providers have been able to block the control meeting. There have also been frequent pat situations. There will be no change in health care as representatives of staff and service providers are aware of their important task. Question 14: No, no, no, In the future, an independent auditor will review the accounts of the institutions. Question 15: The result of an audit on the accounts. On the question 16: Their calculations do not correspond to the results of the elections of the relevant main election commissions. These are the results that have been officially announced. On the question 17: Somebody's got to start. [[Rejoicing and condolences with representatives of FPÖ and ÖVP.]] On the question 18: In this context, I refer to the answer to question 16. I also respect the democratic election results of the Chambers, no matter how they may be. Klaus Uwe Feichtinger:... rotates the minister every six months!]] On question 21: In addition to the previous replies, I can add that German social security has very good experience with it. On the question 22: The answer to this question is not in my area of competence. On the question 23: The preparations are to be made where most know-how is available. Question 24: The umbrella association still has a coordinating function and can decide for itself on its tasks. On question 25: This conclusion is not applicable. We're holding on to the system d'Hondt. The reduction in the number of functionaries is the objective of structural reform and is based on the reduction of the number of insurance companies. On the question 26: The posts of future senior social security staff shall be publicly advertised, as provided for in the law. On the question 27: Of course, the Austrian Health Fund will take regional needs into account in consultation with the Landesbörste. On the question 28: Regional specificities will continue to be taken into account in the future. In addition, I can stress repeatedly that the Austrian Health Fund is legal successor to the local health telephone. On questions 29, 30 and 31: These are again questions that should contribute to the uncertainty of the population. The fact that the procurement of health products and services is to be announced in Europe in the future is not correct. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Noll.]] On questions 32 and 33: This study was not provided to me by Professor Schneider, so I cannot rate it. However, according to media presentation, it is based purely on possible assumptions. On questions 37, 38 and 38a: Several experts have gained their knowledge in the framework of structural reform. At this point also my personal thanks to the employees of my house. Question 39: No. Questions 40, 40a and 40b: No wage tax audits are currently carried out by BVA, SVA and SVB within the framework of the GPLA. This principle is adhered to. On questions 41, 41a and 41b: A joint audit is already being carried out, and the pooling of financial resources is intended to further increase efficiency. On question 42: I assume that the current legal situation will be respected. Social security has a right to encourage examinations. On questions 43, 44, 45 and 46: I refer to the answer to parliamentary questions NR 852/J, NR 306/J, NR 1473/J. On question 47: This question must be asked to the Ministry of Finance. Question 48: 250 full-time equivalents per organization, so a total of 500 nationally. Question 49: In 2016, full-time equivalents were working as GPLA testers in the area’s health telephone and VAEB 52.89. The question of the number of GPLA experts in the financial sector must be asked to the Ministry of Finance. Question 50: I cannot confirm the circumstances described. On question 51: There will be no loss of quality. On questions 52, 53 and 54: Within the meaning of the Federal Law on the Review of Wage-based Fees and Contributions, which was also decided in the Council of Ministers today, the Social Insurance Audit for the ÖGK is carried out by the Financial Audit Service. On question 53: As far as employees who are not subject to the ASVG are concerned, the display is carried out by the competence centre LSD-BG at the ÖGK as before. For employees within the meaning of the ASVG, the audit service of the financial sector shall provide the guidance. On question 54: The audit service shall determine the sub-remuneration and shall also provide the indication. On question 55: There will be neither a loss of control nor a distortion of competition. Each decree, ladies and gentlemen, is the guide to a law in which the competent authority can read out the practical application of the law. Especially in this complex legislative matter, in which rarely one case is exactly the same as the other, the adoption is of crucial importance, for we could never write the legislative text so detailed that each case is undoubtedly illustrated in it. This offer was also gladly accepted by the disabled. In addition, we agree to an accompanying evaluation of the law and a reporting after a year. All of this is not just promises, but we keep that in a resolution which my colleague Kira Grünberg later will bring in. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to conclude by thanking our Lady Minister and her house for having responded quickly and clearly to the situation and for bringing this matter to a repair. We take all the concerns and fears of those concerned very seriously, and today we still have our hand in the joint decision of this law, which we are convinced that it brings legal certainty to the families concerned. I hope that our hand, which we give you with deep conviction, will not be beaten away from you and that we can take a joint decision here where By the uncertainty of the families and persons who are truly hard to deal with, today can be finally ended.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e24707	Mrs Rebecca Kirchbaumer is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e24736	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Values Advisors! Whether you are flying from Innsbruck Kranebitten Airport to Dublin or Crete, in both cases you are subject to the rules of the International Civil Aviation Organization ICAO. Since 1948, Austria has been a member of this United Nations Special Organization. It is based on the Chicago Agreement on International Civil Aviation and sets the standards for aviation worldwide. Among other things, it was the ICAO that standardized and unified the rules on the photos in passports worldwide. In particular, this standardisation has contributed significantly to aviation safety. Since ICAO represents the global community of states – currently it has 192 members – it is only useful to ensure that it is heard enough. There are currently only three countries worldwide which are not members of ICAO, of which two have no airport, namely Liechtenstein and the Vatican. This amendment now ratifys the necessary increase in the number of members of the Council from 36 to 40 and the number of members of the Aviation Commission from 19 to 21. Ladies and gentlemen! Over the past 20 years, many countries have not only been renewed or regained in Europe. This is also to be reflected in the number of members of the bodies. The Council and the Commission shall, inter alia, draw up and establish mandatory standards for aviation to be implemented by the Member States, the rules governing international transport rights, the freedoms of the air, the allocation of ICAO codes for countries and aircraft types and the further development of standards for machine-readable travel documents. This amendment can therefore only be accepted. – Thank you for your attention.

But at least these are huge amounts of data that come to us here. We will also need it for autonomous driving, for autonomous flying. Again: It's changing everything. What we have done, namely that we want to provide all the capitals with 5G by 2020, by 2023 all the main transport connections and by 2025 Austria across the board, are very ambitious targets and this is not so easy to implement. That is why we also need these novelties that we decide on today. We have considered how we can make it easier for entrepreneurs to attach the antennas. The 5G antennas are much smaller than those antennas that you have known so far from the field of mobile phones. These are antennas up to 0.03 cubic meters of room size. We have determined that the public hand must make its property available, i.e. all properties of the ÖBB, all properties of the Asfinag, the Federal Forest, every municipal office, the land ownership, the property of BIG and Belig. All over there, it must be possible to install these antennas; of course, against charges, but there it must be possible. ÖBB and Asfinag are therefore so essential because, if we want to provide the main traffic routes, we need our 5G antennas exactly there, on these routes. The way in which we want to grant frequencies – which is very low, with a very low minimum bid of 30 million euros – has been addressed. But this only concerns the first auction now in the spring in the range between 3.4 and 3.8 gigahertz. The next big auction, which will then come to us, will cost a little more. We just don't want to pay back as exorbitant sums as they were the last time, because in the end the economy also needs the financial resources to be able to roll out the technology. If we also want to provide rural areas, then the technology must not be too expensive. Otherwise, a possible gain in rural areas with less population density will be difficult. That was our way. However, we will also allow multiple use of frequencies. There will be a limitation of minimum bids for frequency auctions. Mrs Himmelbauer has already mentioned it: When a frequency is increased, it must also be used. The technology has to be offered. You can't put any claims here, get something up and then don't put it into practice. That's not going to happen with this law. The amateur radios have made us quite, very big headbreaks, not because, as I should say, there would have been great substantive differences, but because the negotiations have made themselves very difficult. It was so that my employees negotiated, were in conversation, then a ready paper was there, we incorporated these proposals into the law, and then the representative of the community came and said he must, unfortunately, withdraw, he cannot agree as much as we originally did. The consideration was then, whether we take everything out of the law again or whether we say: No matter, we agreed, it remains with these reliefs for amateur radios. These reliefs are still to be found in the legislative text. If I may give examples: The award of special call marks will be made by the Telecommunication Office in the future, without intermediate representatives from the BMVIT. Amateur radio stations can be remotely controlled; that will be possible in the future. A connection with the Internet will be possible. In future, not only radio-amateurs will be allowed to deliver greetings under certain conditions. One point I would like to address, namely the electronic billing. I already know – there were also conversations with the post office – that the post is not very happy with the fact that there is no longer this mandatory paper bill. Here too, however, economic opportunities are changing. I was in Spielberg some time ago at an electric car show and exhibition. There was the mail with its electric vehicles used. For example, the post will now ensure direct transport of the products to customers for a large organic vegetable grower from Styria. These are the new business areas that the post will serve in the future. The letter business just gets less. That's what I'm saying as an old stamp collector. This will simply be less and will simply not be able to play the role as we might have been used to it ten years ago. If you want a paper account, you can continue to obtain it. Even those who want to have a single bill, going back over many years, can get it all. This is all possible, but the mandatory paper bill will no longer exist. I just wish we could use this law to enable rapid 5G expansion, because I am firmly convinced that it is not only a huge opportunity for the urban area, with smart cities and with all the possibilities that will come, but that it is a real and great opportunity for rural areas. There, too, in the rural area, more companies will settle again, because a prerequisite for these companies today is fast internet. Businesses will settle there, and this will also increase jobs in rural areas. This means that there will also be another host in the village, that there will also be a food dealer again, and so on and so on. This means that this land flight is over and that there are also fewer commuters, because most of the commuters I know don't commute because they are so excited because they like to sit in the car and spend a lot of money for fun, but because they don't find a job where they live. I hope that we can also contribute to the increase in quality of life in rural areas, but that the possibility of working there is also greater than it has been.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e26391	The next item is the speech by Mr Kumpitsch. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e26412	Madam President. Mr. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) My previous speakers have already highlighted essential aspects and effects of this new cable car law. I would like to look at some of the more technical details: What was the purpose of this law? – The aim was to ensure and guarantee a smooth procedure and an authorisation system in the future. A special change is that one introduces a separation of concession and technical service life of a cable car system. So far, it has been true that cable car systems, which are different, have had different concession periods of 30, 40 and 50 years. This is now being regulated uniformly at the age of 50. At the same time, the introduction of a so-called general revision takes place. It is true that there will no longer be any technical review in the event of a concession extension, but 40 years after an approval has been granted, a so-called general revision will then be carried out every 30 years. This revision applies to all cable car systems, whether public or private, except for the chair lifts which have already experienced a corresponding regulation. It is also particularly important that there is a point which my predecessor has referred to, that is to say, the withdrawal of the suspensive effect of complaints against a notice granting a building permit or a works permit. Why? – Unfortunately, it occurs again and again – and this has already been described – that complaints are maliciously raised and that this leads to delays. This novice has now been able to stop this dispassion, but at the same time the property owners and the arrants are given the possibility that they can nevertheless file a complaint in individual cases. There has also been an intensification of criminal provisions in certain cases. Let me give you an example: Suppose someone develops a subsystem for a cable car, and this does not comply with this Regulation, then there is the possibility to impose a fine of up to 50 000 euros on that company or legal entity. One has to comply with the legal provisions, and it is true that one has introduced a new criminal record – when someone stops his operation and begins his assignment but does not receive approval for it. Summa summarum must be said that it is a good law. It also shows how this federal government works, because it is already seen that it is very possible to maintain the legal, procedural, technical regulations on the one hand, and on the other hand, to remember the economic aspects and, above all, to guarantee safety for the users of these cable cars.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e28091	Mr President. Madam President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to draw attention to the Report by the Court of Auditors on the internal control system in the case of direct contracts. The aim of the review between April and July 2017 was to review the implementation of recommendations concerning the optimization, control and control of procurement processes at the Federal Chancellery, the Federal Ministry of Finance, the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology and the Federal Ministry of Science, Research and Economy. Already in a preliminary report, the Court of Auditors proposed to the Federation to optimize procurement to set up a standardised federal uniform controlling system so that planning, management and control can be made possible within the respective department at the level of individual procurement groups. In addition, the Court of Auditors has recommended that binding standards and checklists for procurement should be established in order to support the legitimate and appropriate execution and a clear and complete documentation of contracts. The Court of Auditors now found that the Federal Ministry of Finance and the Federal Chancellery did not implement the recommendations of the preliminary report on a uniform procurement control. In line with the preliminary report, the Court of Auditors therefore reaffirmed its recommendation that a standardised sector-wide controlling should be set up, which allows management and control within the respective department at the level of individual procurement groups. Ladies and gentlemen, the Court of Auditors' report on the internal control system in the field of direct procurement is objective and detailed, and this is why the report should also be approved. All bodies will benefit from a standardized national controlling in the field of direct procurement, and we can also create more transparency. This is very welcome in my eyes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e29203	Thank you for bringing me in. – Mr President, I should like to thank you for your support. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! At first glance, he is relatively unspectacular, it is about administrative questions, but when you look at them, you see that this is actually a very large dimension. What are UID numbers? – UID numbers are registration numbers that companies need to be able to claim on the one hand pre-tax deduction and to invoice with VAT. In the case of cross-border trade in goods and services, companies need UID numbers if they want to exempt VAT; both the senders of the goods and the receivers of the goods must have a UID number. If we now know – and look at the reports of the European Commission – that the issue of VAT fraud at the European level is a very big one – the European Commission estimates the failure at around EUR 50 billion a year – then this issue of the UID numbers is very, very important. There is an expertise of the Court of Auditors, which is brought in in close cooperation with the BMF. The examination we are debating today was also a follow-up review, during which the BMF and the financial offices in Salzburg have been reviewed. And there has already been a lot of progress together, and some of the Court's recommendations have been implemented. But it is also of vital importance – and we have already discussed this with other agenda items – that, especially during the EU Presidency of Austria, the issues of profit-shifting, tax fraud are at the top of the agenda. As a result, the Court of Auditors' recommendations could be included in the European Council, the informal Ecofin Council, and there is a clear common objective of genuinely vehemently acting against this VAT fraud. Initial successes have also been achieved here. Technical implementation is the subject of this issue. I should also like to thank the Court of Auditors for their expertise. Fighting VAT fraud at European level is certainly a common goal, and the Court of Auditors report was certainly a good basis for us to move forward. – Thank you very much.

The Federal Gardens comprise seven of Austria's most valuable historical gardens and garden avenues: the former Habsburg gardens in Vienna and Innsbruck. These include the Augarten, the Belvederegarten, the Burggarten, the Volksgarten and the Schlosspark Schönbrunn in Vienna as well as the Hofgarten Innsbruck and the Schlosspark Ambras. In 2016, the Austrian Federal Gardens were merged with the Higher Federal Institute for Horticulture and Research, which is responsible for teaching and research in the field of horticulture. The Court of Auditors strongly criticises this kind of merger between the two departments. It brought almost no structural changes, the operational problems were not solved. Despite the organisational aggregation of the administration, which, while saving the position of the director, has even slightly increased the level of staffing. My previous speaker has already responded to the many other recommendations, so I would like to say: Even if all these recommendations of the Court of Auditors are implemented and the administration works better, it is worth taking a look abroad and seeing how historical gardens are managed there. Historical gardens are mostly connected in the organization of castle administrations, since castle and garden are often a design whole, especially in the Baroque. In Germany, the working group of castles and gardens works for the preservation of historical gardens; in addition, there are numerous private foundations that operate with a high financial effort. In the UK, the historical gardens are of particular interest in an official register, in accordance with the National Heritage Act. But also in France, Italy, Spain and the Netherlands, throughout Europe, one is aware of the treasure of the historical gardens and facilities. That is why the International Committee on Historical Gardens, Icomos-IFLA, made the following decision in the Charter of Florence in 1981: “Historical gardens belong to the elements of the cultural heritage, whose survival naturally requires an extreme care by qualified persons”, namely “by historians, architects, landscape architects, gardeners and botanists”. We therefore see that the preservation of historical gardens goes far beyond good and efficient administration, as the Court of Auditors recommends and which is also indispensable. I am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that our new minister, Elisabeth Köstinger, is aware of this task and that she draws particular attention to the historical gardens and gardens. - Thank you.

One of the most important achievements of the First Republic in the years following its foundation was the construction of municipal apartments, especially in the Red Vienna of the 1920s – but not only, but also in other cities. By 1934, 61 175 apartments were built in 348 residential buildings and 5 227 apartments in 42 rowing houses. In 1934 each tenth Vienna and every tenth Viennan lived in a municipal building. These measures, which were adopted a hundred years ago, are still noticeable to everyone or to many of us today, because by mixing municipal apartments with private homes in all districts in Vienna, but also in other cities, the rents remain stable. Why has Vienna been elected nine times in a row to the world's most vital city since 2009? – Among other things also due to the cost of living, and there municipal apartments play an essential role. ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e31137	Finally, we reach the 30th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. First of all, Mr Zinggl, you have the floor. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-24-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00043_d7e31164	The reason for my own-initiative proposal, which we would like to discuss very briefly at first reading, is the observation that there are still parliamentary questions put to false ministers, ministers. That means they're two months away and then the answer comes: The Minister questioned, the Minister requested, would not be responsible. I do not think it has anything to do with being too stupid and the wrong minister who asks the wrong minister – no one would ask the Minister of the Interior anything about the Spanish court riding school – but it has more to do with the fact that there are overlapping areas and therefore one does not know exactly which minister is responsible. There are also legal texts in which a Federal Chancellor is present, who is responsible but not operationally responsible. So there are overlaps that sometimes make it not so easy for Members to recognize it correctly. In any event, it is not necessary to allow two months to pass, in order to then ask the questioner the task of submitting his request again. Therefore, my proposal: As the incoming requests are examined very quickly anyway, they should be returned to the questioner after seven days, and in this way the answer could be accelerated. - Thank you.
